  8. The Veil: Is its bloodline filter permanent in practice, or could a future researcher discover a dangerous bypass despite the missing reversal spell?
  9. The breach: What exact action did the Oathbreaker take after the sword manifested, what opened or failed, and who survived to name him an Oathbreaker?
  10. The inheritance: Which souls qualify as bloodline-accessible, and how many unrelated fallen-kingdom citizens remain archived but permanently inaccessible to him?

One-sentence premise: Across an Earth-centered network of Rifts, Faith empowers entities that can exploit believers; the Queen’s pre-death Preservation overrides a royal Retention curse, provoking the domain-bound Archivist to manifest as the wielder-driven Codex during a breach caused by an oath-bound knight who must then seek atonement.

The_Rift_Convergence_Core_Story_Entities_and_Cosmic_Principles_v1_0.md ARCHIVE

Historical source; may contain superseded terminology or proposals.

THE RIFT CONVERGENCE

Core Story, Ancient Entities & Cosmic Principles

World Codex — v1.0

Scope: A standalone reference containing only the overarching story, ancient entities, and cosmic/spiritual principles. Character biography, combat systems, card mechanics, and detailed Oathbreaker progression are intentionally excluded.

Canon priority: The latest Faith retcon governs this document: only X / the Coalition harnesses Faith. Will is a distinct, inward source of power, not a form of Faith; neither the Queen nor the Archivist draws power from Faith.

Status: Established means accepted canon; Working means not yet finalized; Open means unresolved.

I. CORE STORY

The Rift Convergence

Long before the modern era, civilizations existed in isolation across separate worlds. Decades before the game's present, interdimensional Rifts appeared, with Earth serving as the crossroads of the modern network: known modern Rifts connect other worlds to Earth rather than directly to one another.

Contact brought exchange of cultures, technologies, resources, and magic, but also rivalry and conflict. The interworld Accord was formed to preserve coexistence. Its member civilizations eventually became entangled in wars, and key Accord leaders fell under secret Coalition manipulation.

The Rifts are passages, not the source of Faith or Will. The heroes' larger task is to address threats within connected worlds, not indiscriminately seal every Rift.

X and the manufactured salvation

X / the Coalition is a small group of ancient, powerful beings associated with a founding Rift world. The Coalition engineers instability, suffering, and war, then offers rescue so frightened populations voluntarily place genuine Faith in its members.

  • Faith amplifies the Coalition's existing supernatural capacities; it cannot be manufactured by simply ordering someone to believe.
  • Soul Consumption destroys individual souls irreversibly and sustains the Coalition's longevity.
  • Its apparent utopia is constructed from the remains of deceased followers, masking exploitation beneath promises of salvation.
  • X condemns Will as dangerous, impure, or selfish because self-directed power undermines dependence on external saviors.

The ancient kingdom and the eradication of Will

An ancient human kingdom existed centuries or millennia before the modern Convergence. Through an exceptional, small precursor Rift [Working], its early ruler encountered the Archivist in the form of an old man, who revealed the existence of Will through teaching rather than creating it.

Generations of scholars studied Will, souls, and royal spiritual inheritance, developing Ancestral Dominion. Their knowledge threatened X's ideology: individuals could become stronger through their own conviction instead of surrendering belief to the Coalition.

During the first chaos of the modern Rifts, X launched an intentional genocide against the kingdom and those who practiced, taught, or protected Will. The attack preceded the Sovereign Veil and was not caused by it.

The Sovereign Veil: the last contingency

Only in the kingdom's final hours did the reigning king, his family, and surviving scholars attempt the Sovereign Veil: an unfinished, untested spiritual construct layered over the Archivist's innate Retention.

Design element Purpose and effect
Concealment Hide the Archivist, the Ancestral Domain, and their spiritual connections from X.
Bloodline-only future Retention Reduce the detectable activity of soul collection by limiting future eligible transfers to the designated royal bloodline.
Restricted inheritance access Prevent royal heirs from freely reaching the Archivist's unrelated, pre-Veil collection.
Ancestral transfer Route eligible bloodline souls into Retention at death, unless a prior incompatible Preservation Condition takes precedence.
Unintended concealment The Veil also hides the Domain from the Queen, although she was not its intended target.
  • The emergency initially retained souls of the dying kingdom, including some people outside the royal bloodline; later collection was bloodline-restricted.
  • The Veil did not injure the Archivist, erase his original archive, or supply him with a new innate power.
  • The construct lacked tested redundancy, failover, and a completed reversal method.

The Queen's colony and the Champions

A Rift reveals an unusual insectoid colony whose ordinary subjects are soulless. Their nature resists the Coalition's conventional soul-targeting influence, prompting the Accord to declare a truce against the colony.

The Queen preserves selected living individuals for reasons tied to her undisclosed ambitions. When preserved Champions die, she can Restore them, creating the narrative basis for repeated expeditions and returns to the colony.

Her selections are opportunistic rather than proof of moral approval. She can observe distant places through her colony but cannot see through the Sovereign Veil or recover souls already retained by the Archivist.

The collision of ancient conditions

During the Oathbreaker's hometown crisis, the Queen establishes Preservation on his soul while he is still alive. That condition takes precedence over the Sovereign Veil's pending death-triggered Retention claim.

The Archivist responds to the threatened claim by manifesting as The Codex, a physical conduit that cannot act without a wielder. The knight's own uncontrolled use of the newly accessible inherited power causes the catastrophic breach; the precise mechanism remains Open.

This incident connects the Queen's preservation, the Archivist's retention, the concealed royal inheritance, and the ongoing conflict surrounding X without making either ancient entity directly responsible for the knight's choices.

Chronology

Era Major event
Distant past The Archivist selects and retains knowledgeable souls from many civilizations in The Library.
Ancient kingdom The old-man Archivist teaches a ruler about Will; generations of spiritual research follow.
Early modern Convergence Earth-centered Rifts emerge; X exploits the chaos to eradicate the kingdom and knowledge of Will.
Kingdom's final hours The scholars and royal family activate the unfinished Sovereign Veil and conceal the Ancestral Domain.
Later generations Eligible royal descendants survive in scattered, often forgotten branches.
Breach incident The Queen's living-soul Preservation conflicts with pending Retention; the Archivist manifests as The Codex.
Game present Champions travel the connected worlds as the Coalition's manipulations and the Queen's goals remain unresolved.

II. ANCIENT ENTITIES

X / The Coalition

Attribute Canon
Nature A small group of ancient beings, with X as its central antagonist or ideological force.
Power source Faith received through genuine belief from followers; exclusively harnessed by this group.
Longevity Consumption of souls, which permanently destroys spiritual continuity.
Method Orchestrates crises and offers salvation to cultivate dependence and belief.
Ideology Condemns Will because self-reliance threatens the Coalition's power structure.
Unresolved Membership, exact origins, ultimate plans, and relative strength versus the Queen.

The Queen

An ancient, abstract insectoid matriarch whose powers are innate, not derived from Faith. Her authority spans the creation of life, the Preservation of living souls, and the Restoration of souls she has already preserved.

Ability Rule
Life creation Creates soulless insectoid subjects; she is not established as a creator of souls from nothing.
Preservation Establishes a restorable condition while the subject is alive.
Restoration Returns a previously preserved soul to embodied life after death.
Collective Sight — The Thousand Eyes Observes through biologically linked subjects that scout, forage, and survive like members of an ant colony.
Passive awareness Receives basic impressions of movement, danger, and unusual events across her colony.
Focused sight Directs attention through an individual subject's available senses.
Collective observation Combines scouting across many subjects to search for useful potential Champions.

Limits: The Queen is not omniscient; her scouts must physically reach places and cannot inherently read hidden souls or penetrate the Veil. She cannot retroactively preserve or restore an already-retained soul, and her subjects do not supply Faith.

The Archivist

An incomprehensibly ancient entity whose innate domain is Retention. It collects real, conscious souls without preserving their ability to return to life.

Aspect Canon
The Library Its original, pre-Veil collection of knowledgeable souls gathered across civilizations.
Retention Stores individual identities, memories, and consciousness indefinitely without restorability.
Old-man manifestation Influences the physical world indirectly through knowledge, counsel, and direction.
The Codex manifestation A physical conduit requiring another individual to wield it.
The Ancestral Domain The kingdom's concealed spiritual inheritance under the Veil's restrictions.
Veil limitation Future Retention is bloodline-restricted; the original Library remains intact but inaccessible to the royal inheritor.

Limits: The Archivist cannot independently wage physical campaigns, wield its own sword, or Restore retained souls. The Veil is an imposed conditional overlay, not damage to its essential Retention power.

Entity comparison

Entity Innate sphere Influence on the physical world Relationship to Faith
X / Coalition Soul manipulation and Consumption Manipulates rulers, followers, bodies, and conflicts Only group that harnesses Faith
Queen Life creation, Preservation, Restoration Directly through colony, scouts, and restored Champions Does not use Faith
Archivist Retention and accumulated knowledge Indirectly through counsel and a wielder-dependent sword Does not use Faith

Hierarchy note: The Queen takes precedence over the Archivist in the established Preservation-versus-Retention conflict because her condition attaches to the living soul before the Archivist's death-triggered transfer. This does not establish an unconditional combat ranking against X.

III. COSMIC PRINCIPLES & SPIRITUAL LAWS

Foundational concepts

Principle Definition Boundary
Faith Genuine belief from followers that X / the Coalition alone harnesses to amplify existing supernatural powers. Cannot be forced into authenticity; does not automatically create new innate abilities.
Will Independent, inward conviction that strengthens one's own soul and its existing capabilities. Not inward Faith under the latest retcon; does not automatically grant soul command or resurrection.
Soul The real spiritual identity and consciousness of an individual. A retained soul is a person, not a recording or imitation.
Spiritual continuity The ongoing identity and, where preserved, the capacity for future embodiment. Continuity of identity does not necessarily mean restorability.
Ancestral Dominion A hereditary authority developed within the ancient royal line to command authorized retained souls. Not an automatic property of Will; does not grant access to The Library.

Four distinct soul operations

Operation Associated entity Effect on soul Identity remains? Can return to life?
Consume X / Coalition Permanently destroys spiritual identity and continuity. No No
Retain Archivist Stores the conscious soul indefinitely but without restorable continuity. Yes No
Preserve Queen Protects a living soul's capacity for future restoration. Yes Yes
Restore Queen Returns an already-preserved soul to a living body. Yes Already restored

Precedence of conditions

  1. While alive: The Queen can establish a Preservation Condition on an eligible living soul.
  2. At death: If that condition is already active, it takes precedence over the Veil's pending Retention transfer for that soul.
  3. After death: The Queen can Restore a preserved soul, but cannot retroactively Preserve or Restore a soul that has already entered Retention.
  4. After Consumption: The soul's identity and continuity are destroyed; neither Retention nor Restoration can recover it under established rules.

Governing distinctions

  • Faith and Will are distinct: The Coalition alone harnesses Faith; Will is independent personal conviction, not a lesser expression of Faith.
  • Innate authority is not interchangeable: Faith amplifies the Coalition's abilities, while the Queen's and Archivist's capacities arise from their own natures.
  • Concealment is not preservation: The Sovereign Veil hides the Domain; it does not make retained souls restorable.
  • Retention is not restoration: Souls within The Library or Ancestral Domain remain conscious but cannot be brought back by the Queen.
  • Collective Sight is not omniscience: The Queen sees through living subjects rather than automatically detecting all spiritual activity.

IV. UNRESOLVED WORLD-LEVEL QUESTIONS

  • Who founded the Coalition, and what are X's ultimate intentions beyond power and longevity?
  • How did X identify and systematically eradicate practitioners of Will?
  • Does X suspect the Archivist or concealed Ancestral Domain survived?
  • What is the Queen's long-term objective in selecting and repeatedly restoring Champions?
  • Did the Archivist willingly accept the Sovereign Veil, or was its restriction imposed against its wishes?
  • Can the Veil ever be safely reversed, given that its original designers left no completed reversal?
  • How exactly does the Queen select and establish Preservation on a distant living subject?

Document boundary: This volume deliberately omits the Oathbreaker's detailed personal history, family dynamics, equipment design, and card/hex-board gameplay. Those belong in the separate character and gameplay references.

THE RIFT CONVERGENCE

Core World Codex

Core Story, Ancient Entities & Cosmic Principles — v1.1

Scope: World-level story, factions, ancient entities unrelated to the Oathbreaker's personal lineage, and universal cosmic laws. The Ancient Kingdom, Archivist, The Codex, Sovereign Veil, Ancestral Domain, The Library, and Ancestral Dominion belong in the separate Oathbreaker Lore volume.

Canon priority: Only X / the Coalition harnesses Faith. Will is an independent inward power, not a subdivision of Faith. The Queen's powers are innate.

Status: Established = accepted canon; Working = not finalized; Open = unresolved.

I. CORE STORY

The Rift Convergence

  • Rifts appeared decades before the game's present, connecting otherwise isolated worlds through Earth, the central crossroads of the modern network.
  • Contact enabled exchanges of culture, technology, magic, and resources, but escalating rivalries led to interworld wars.
  • The Accord formed to preserve coexistence among connected civilizations; influential leaders were secretly manipulated by the Coalition.
  • Heroes travel into connected worlds to resolve crises, not necessarily to close every Rift.

X and Manufactured Salvation

  • X / the Coalition is a small group of ancient, powerful beings associated with a founding Rift world.
  • The Coalition secretly engineers war, suffering, and instability, then presents itself as salvation to cultivate genuine Faith in its members.
  • Faith amplifies the Coalition's existing supernatural capabilities, but genuine belief cannot simply be commanded into existence.
  • Soul Consumption irreversibly destroys souls to sustain the Coalition's longevity.
  • Its apparent utopia is built from the physical remains of dead followers, concealing exploitation beneath a promise of paradise.
  • X condemns Will as impure, selfish, or dangerous because independent spiritual strength threatens the Coalition's ideology of dependence.

The Queen's Colony

  • A Rift reveals an ancient insectoid Queen and her colony of soulless subjects.
  • The colony resists the Coalition's ordinary soul-targeting methods, and the Accord declares a truce to oppose it.
  • The Queen's motivations are unknown; she selects potentially useful Champions for her own purposes rather than on moral merit.
  • The Queen establishes Preservation while a selected subject is alive, enabling eventual Restoration if the subject dies.
  • Her colony functions as the hub to which preserved Champions can return following a failed expedition.

Champions and the Rebirth Loop

Element World-level role
Champions Soul-bearing individuals selected and preserved by the Queen.
Preservation Established on a living soul before death.
Restoration Returns an already-preserved soul to embodied life after death.
Linked souls Explain recovery from near death while a party remains active.
Party defeat When the entire party falls, the Queen restores preserved Champions at the colony.
Agency Preservation does not force obedience, gratitude, or genuine Faith.

The Queen

  • The Queen is an ancient, abstract insectoid matriarch with innate powers over life creation, Preservation, and Restoration.
  • Her own soul does not make her a Faith user under the current canon; only the Coalition harnesses Faith.
  • Her ordinary insectoid subjects are soulless and therefore cannot grant Faith or be targeted by the Coalition's usual soul-based methods.
  • She does not have an established ability to create souls from nothing.

Governing Laws

  • Only the Coalition harnesses Faith. Others may possess souls or genuine convictions without receiving Faith-based supernatural amplification.

The_Rift_Convergence_Story_Sanctum_and_Sovereignty_v2_0.md ARCHIVE

Historical source; may contain superseded terminology or proposals.

THE RIFT CONVERGENCE

Story — Sanctum & Sovereignty Edition

Version: 2.0
Status: Consolidated story and Oathbreaker retcon
Terminology: The Stewards replace X / the Coalition. Faith is removed from this story's supernatural system.

Canon priority: The latest decisions about the Stewards, Sanctum, Sovereignty, Will, and the Oathbreaker supersede older Faith-based explanations. [Established] indicates specified canon; [Open] indicates mechanics or events not yet settled.

I. Core Story

Decades ago, Rifts began opening between worlds, with Earth serving as a crossroads in the modern convergence. The connected civilizations formed the Accord, initially sharing technology, magic, resources, and people. Tensions eventually escalated into a devastating interworld war.

The war was engineered. One of the Accord's founding worlds is ruled by the Stewards, a small group who secretly control the Accord's leaders. Their goal is to make other worlds desperate enough to willingly accept their rule and lower their souls' defenses, allowing the Stewards to siphon strength and sustain themselves by consuming souls.

A new Rift reveals the Queen's primitive, insectoid colony. Its soulless subjects resist the Stewards' ordinary soul-based influence. The Accord calls a truce against the colony; heroes from formerly hostile worlds meet on that front, and the Queen selects Champions to oppose the Stewards for reasons of her own.

Core factions

Faction / place Role
The Accord Interworld alliance, compromised through leaders controlled by the Stewards.
The Stewards Rulers who use asymmetrical Siphoning and soul Consumption to sustain power and lifespan.
The Queen's colony Soulless insectoid society; her scouts extend her perception across the world.
Champions Soul-bearing individuals selected by the Queen for Preservation and eventual Restoration.
Rifts Pathways between worlds; the modern network converges through Earth.

II. Cosmic Principles — Souls, Sanctum, and Sovereignty

Term Definition
Soul An individual's spiritual identity and consciousness.
Sanctum The protective barrier surrounding a soul.
Sovereignty A soul's authority over itself, protected by its Sanctum.
Will Inward conviction and disciplined spiritual exertion; the Oathbreaker uses it to pressure another soul's Sanctum.
Siphoning Drawing strength through a connection between souls. For ordinary souls, the exchange is reciprocal.
Consumption Permanent destruction of a soul for the Stewards' continued lifespan.
Retention The Archivist's keeping of real, conscious souls without their capacity for bodily restoration.
Preservation The Queen's protection of a living soul's capacity for later restoration.
Restoration The Queen's return of an already-preserved soul to embodied life.

Sanctum rules [Established]

  • Every ordinary soul has a Sanctum that protects its Sovereignty.
  • To receive strength through ordinary Siphoning, a soul must lower its own Sanctum, exposing itself to reciprocal exchange.
  • Ordinary Siphoning is mutual and generally limited to a small number of close souls.
  • A lowered Sanctum can expose its owner to manipulation, loss of Sovereignty, and—if lowered sufficiently—alteration of the soul and body.
  • No individual can simply force someone to choose to lower their Sanctum.
  • Faith is not part of this cosmology. Earlier descriptions of Faith as the universal power or of Will as inward Faith are retired.

Will and Sovereignty [Established direction]

Will is the Oathbreaker's means of exerting spiritual pressure against a retained soul's Sanctum. When he successfully overcomes its protection, he can assert control over a measure of that soul's Sovereignty, compelling its skills or abilities through Ancestral Dominion.

This is not Siphoning: he does not gain the soul's strength through a mutually opened exchange, and he does not need that soul's consent. The soul remains an individual, conscious and potentially resistant; command does not imply loyalty, forgiveness, or belief.

[Open] The precise thresholds, duration, resistance, and consequences of forcing a retained soul's Sanctum are not yet defined. The established bloodline access limits still apply.

III. The Stewards — The Stripped

The Stewards are a small group from one founding world. They belong to the same race as their subjects but are born with complete souls without Sanctums; only their race can produce such individuals.

Aspect Rule
Innate difference Born without a Sanctum; also called the Stripped.
Siphoning Can draw from souls that lower their Sanctums without exposing themselves in return.
Force Can forcibly breach raised Sanctums, but only on a small scale; the Accord's leaders are among their controlled targets.
Control More freely lowered Sanctums allow greater influence over many followers.
Transformation Sufficiently lowered Sanctums permit changes to souls and, consequently, bodies.
Consumption Permanently destroys followers' souls to extend the Stewards' lives.
Political method Manufacture war and desperation to persuade populations to accept their rule voluntarily.
Homeworld An apparent utopia whose buildings are constructed from the remains of consumed followers.

The Stewards' central vulnerability is consent at scale: they cannot compel entire civilizations to lower their Sanctums, so they engineer circumstances in which people do so willingly. Their ability to forcibly breach a few raised Sanctums does not grant unlimited coercive reach.

IV. The Queen and Her Colony

The Queen is an ancient, soul-bearing insectoid entity whose innate abilities include creating soulless insectoid life, Preservation, and Restoration. Her colony functions through independent insect instincts and a biological sensory link to its creator.

Collective Sight — The Thousand Eyes

  • Passive awareness: Insects communicate primitive impressions of movement, threats, and unusual events.
  • Focused sight: The Queen can perceive through a chosen subject's senses.
  • Collective observation: Scouts survey distant regions and help identify possible Champions.
  • Limit: Scouts must physically reach a place; they do not automatically read souls or penetrate the Sovereign Veil.

Soul continuity

  • The Queen establishes Preservation while the subject is alive.
  • At death, a previously preserved soul retains the capacity for Restoration.
  • Souls already Retained by the Archivist cannot be retroactively preserved or restored.
  • Preservation does not automatically grant the Queen the subject's consent, loyalty, or Sovereignty.
  • The Queen's methods do not require physically eating subjects; older descriptions of her consuming bodies to preserve them are retired.

The Queen's motives remain opaque. Her preserved Champions can return after a party wipe, supporting the game's roguelike run structure.

V. The Ancient Kingdom — An Oathbreaker-Related History

The ancient human kingdom arose on an Earth-like planet distinct from modern Earth. Long before its destruction, an anomalous encounter with the Archivist's old-man manifestation introduced its ruler to the principles of Will; the Archivist offered knowledge and direction rather than ruling the kingdom.

Over generations, the kingdom's scholars refined Will and developed the royal inheritance called Ancestral Dominion. This was an extraordinary capacity to assert authority over eligible retained souls, not a form of the Stewards' Siphoning.

The Convergence and genocide

The kingdom's planet was caught in a Rift Convergence event. One of the newly opened Rifts happened to connect it to the Stewards' world, exposing the kingdom to their civilization and, eventually, their ambitions.

The Stewards identified the kingdom's mastery of Will and soul Sovereignty as a threat to their system of control through lowered Sanctums. During the chaos, they launched a deliberate genocide against the kingdom and the people preserving its knowledge. The Sovereign Veil did not cause the attack.

[Open] The exact process by which the Stewards learned of the kingdom's techniques, and whether they initially posed as benefactors, has not been established.

The final contingency

In the kingdom's last hours, the reigning king, royal family, and surviving scholars enacted the unfinished Sovereign Veil. They sought to conceal the Archivist and the Ancestral Domain from the Stewards and save the spiritual inheritance of the royal line.

  • Concealment: Hides the Archivist, Ancestral Domain, and their spiritual connections from the Stewards.
  • Retention filter: Restricts future retention to the designated royal bloodline, reducing detectable spiritual activity.
  • Inheritance filter: Restricts the royal descendant's access to the authorized ancestral inheritance, not the Archivist's older Library.
  • Emergency: The kingdom's dying souls were retained during the final contingency, including some non-royal individuals; this does not automatically grant the Oathbreaker command over them.
  • Limitations: The Veil was untested, unfinished, lacked redundancy, and had no completed reversal spell.

The Queen is also unable to perceive the hidden Domain, an unintended consequence. Even if she could see it, the souls already under Retention lack the continuity needed for Restoration.

VI. The Archivist, the Library, and The Codex

Term Meaning
The Archivist Ancient, domain-bound entity whose innate ability is Retention.
The Library Its original pre-Veil collection of real, conscious, knowledgeable souls.
Ancestral Domain The concealed inheritance associated with the destroyed kingdom and its bloodline.
Sovereign Veil Artificial overlay limiting the Archivist's future retention and concealing the Domain.
The Codex Physical manifestation of the Archivist that requires a wielder to act.
Ancestral Dominion The Oathbreaker's inherited authority to command eligible ancestral souls.

The Archivist cannot directly conduct campaigns in the physical world or wield The Codex independently. Its old-man form influences through teaching; its sword form provides a physical conduit for an eligible wielder.

VII. The Oathbreaker — Story Synopsis

Generations after the ancient kingdom's destruction, a knight descended unknowingly from an illegitimate branch of its royal house. He swore to defend his hometown and family, devoting his life to the protection of those under his care.

During the unfolding catastrophe, the Queen selected the living knight and established a Preservation Condition on his soul. That earlier condition threatened the Veil's pending, death-triggered Retention of his royal bloodline soul. The Archivist responded by manifesting as The Codex, exposing the knight's dormant Ancestral Dominion.

Untrained and overwhelmed, the knight used his newly accessible power recklessly. His own actions caused the breach, destroying his hometown and killing his family; the precise action that triggered the catastrophe remains [Open]. He did not intend to betray his oath, but he was responsible for failing it.

His eligible relatives were retained by the Archivist. They remain conscious, and their silent acts of care—polishing his helmet, maintaining his sword, tending his gear—express familial devotion, not Faith or supernatural power transfer. Many other ancestral souls resist him or resent being compelled.

The knight became known as the Oathbreaker. He can remove his helmet, but refuses to reveal his face in another person's presence because of his shame. Through the Queen's Preservation and Restoration, he survives repeated deaths and journeys through the Rifts in pursuit of atonement.

How the Oathbreaker commands souls [New canon]

  1. Eligibility: The Sovereign Veil permits access only to souls within his authorized ancestral inheritance.
  2. Contact: The Codex provides the conduit through which the Oathbreaker reaches an eligible retained soul.
  3. Pressure: He focuses Will against that soul's Sanctum, meeting resistance from the soul's protection and resolve.
  4. Sovereignty: When he overcomes the Sanctum sufficiently, Ancestral Dominion asserts command over part of the soul's Sovereignty.
  5. Invocation: He compels an eligible skill, technique, or ability into action through the sword.
  6. Consequences: The soul remains conscious and may resent the coercion; the power does not manufacture loyalty or grant access to unrelated souls in the Library.

The central character conflict: The Oathbreaker can force an ancestral soul to act, but cannot make it forgive him. His mastery of Will may make him more capable, yet his redemption depends on what he chooses to do with that power.

VIII. Historical Sequence

Order Event
1 The Archivist retains knowledgeable souls across ancient civilizations, forming The Library.
2 An ancient human ruler encounters the Archivist; generations develop Will and Ancestral Dominion.
3 The kingdom's Earth-like planet is caught in a Rift Convergence; a Rift connects it to the Stewards' world.
4 The Stewards discover the threat posed by the kingdom's spiritual practices and launch a genocide.
5 The king, family, and scholars enact emergency Retention and the unfinished Sovereign Veil.
6 Generations later, a forgotten royal descendant becomes a knight and swears his oath.
7 The Queen preserves him while alive; the Archivist manifests as The Codex.
8 The knight's uncontrolled actions cause the breach, killing his family and earning him the name Oathbreaker.
9 The Oathbreaker travels through the Rifts seeking atonement and refining control over ancestral souls.

IX. Canon Boundaries and Open Questions

Fixed boundaries

  • Stewards, not X or the Coalition, is the current antagonist name.
  • Faith is removed; explanations based on receiving belief from others no longer apply.
  • Sanctum protects Sovereignty; ordinary Siphoning is reciprocal, while the Stewards' is asymmetrical.
  • Will and Ancestral Dominion enable the Oathbreaker to compel eligible retained souls, without implying voluntary cooperation.
  • The Queen preserves living souls, and does not need to eat people to do so.
  • The Archivist's Library remains intact and inaccessible to the Oathbreaker's inherited Dominion.
  • The Ancient Kingdom is on an Earth-like planet, and the Stewards' Rift appeared there during a convergence event.
  • The Oathbreaker caused the breach; the exact triggering act remains unsettled.

Open questions

  1. What determines a retained soul's Sanctum resistance, and how does the Oathbreaker's Will overcome it?
  2. Does compelled Sovereignty last only for one Invocation, or can it persist longer?
  3. Can an eligible soul voluntarily cooperate without its Sanctum being forced, and how would that differ mechanically?
  4. How did the Stewards first recognize the kingdom's Will as a threat?
  5. What exact action by the Oathbreaker caused the breach?
  6. What is the Queen's ultimate goal in selecting and restoring Champions?
  7. Can a lowered Sanctum rise again, and can forced intrusion cause lasting harm?

One-sentence premise: In a universe where the Stewards exploit lowered Sanctums to steal strength and Sovereignty, a disgraced knight wields Will against the Sanctums of his retained ancestors, seeking redemption for the catastrophe he himself caused.

The_Rift_Convergence_Story_Sanctum_and_Sovereignty_v2_1.md ARCHIVE

Historical source; may contain superseded terminology or proposals.

THE RIFT CONVERGENCE

Story — Sanctum & Sovereignty Edition

Version: 2.1
Status: Consolidated story and Oathbreaker retcon
Terminology: The Stewards replace X / the Coalition. Faith is removed from this story's supernatural system.

Canon priority: The latest decisions about the Stewards, Sanctum, Sovereignty, Will, and the Oathbreaker supersede older Faith-based explanations. [Established] indicates specified canon; [Open] indicates mechanics or events not yet settled.

I. Core Story

Decades ago, Rifts began opening unpredictably between different worlds. These pathways connected civilizations that had previously been isolated, without any single world serving as the center of the network. The connected civilizations formed the Accord, initially sharing technology, magic, resources, and people. Tensions eventually escalated into a devastating interworld war.

The war was engineered. One of the Accord's founding worlds is ruled by the Stewards, a small group who secretly control the Accord's leaders. Their goal is to make other worlds desperate enough to willingly accept their rule and lower their souls' defenses, allowing the Stewards to siphon strength and sustain themselves by consuming souls.

A new Rift reveals the Queen's primitive, insectoid colony. Its soulless subjects resist the Stewards' ordinary soul-based influence. The Accord calls a truce against the colony; heroes from formerly hostile worlds meet on that front, and the Queen selects Champions to oppose the Stewards for reasons of her own.

Core factions

Faction / place Role
The Accord Interworld alliance, compromised through leaders controlled by the Stewards.
The Stewards Rulers who use asymmetrical Siphoning and soul Consumption to sustain power and lifespan.
The Queen's colony Soulless insectoid society; her scouts extend her perception across the world.
Champions Soul-bearing individuals selected by the Queen for Preservation and eventual Restoration.
Rifts Unpredictably appearing pathways connecting different worlds; no universal hub world.

II. Cosmic Principles — Souls, Sanctum, and Sovereignty

Term Definition
Soul An individual's spiritual identity and consciousness.
Sanctum The protective barrier surrounding a soul.
Sovereignty A soul's authority over itself, protected by its Sanctum.
Will Inward conviction and disciplined spiritual exertion; the Oathbreaker uses it to pressure another soul's Sanctum.
Siphoning Drawing strength through a connection between souls. For ordinary souls, the exchange is reciprocal.
Consumption Permanent destruction of a soul for the Stewards' continued lifespan.
Retention The Archivist's keeping of real, conscious souls without their capacity for bodily restoration.
Preservation The Queen's protection of a living soul's capacity for later restoration.
Restoration The Queen's return of an already-preserved soul to embodied life.

Sanctum rules [Established]

  • Every ordinary soul has a Sanctum that protects its Sovereignty.
  • To receive strength through ordinary Siphoning, a soul must lower its own Sanctum, exposing itself to reciprocal exchange.
  • Ordinary Siphoning is mutual and generally limited to a small number of close souls.
  • A lowered Sanctum can expose its owner to manipulation, loss of Sovereignty, and—if lowered sufficiently—alteration of the soul and body.
  • No individual can simply force someone to choose to lower their Sanctum.
  • Faith is not part of this cosmology. Earlier descriptions of Faith as the universal power or of Will as inward Faith are retired.

Will and Sovereignty [Established direction]

Will is the Oathbreaker's means of exerting spiritual pressure against a retained soul's Sanctum. When he successfully overcomes its protection, he can assert control over a measure of that soul's Sovereignty, compelling its skills or abilities through Ancestral Dominion.

This is not Siphoning: he does not gain the soul's strength through a mutually opened exchange, and he does not need that soul's consent. The soul remains an individual, conscious and potentially resistant; command does not imply loyalty, forgiveness, or belief.

[Open] The precise thresholds, duration, resistance, and consequences of forcing a retained soul's Sanctum are not yet defined. The established bloodline access limits still apply.

III. The Stewards — The Stripped

The Stewards are a small group from one founding world. They belong to the same race as their subjects but are born with complete souls without Sanctums; only their race can produce such individuals.

Aspect Rule
Innate difference Born without a Sanctum; also called the Stripped.
Siphoning Can draw from souls that lower their Sanctums without exposing themselves in return.
Force Can forcibly breach raised Sanctums, but only on a small scale; the Accord's leaders are among their controlled targets.
Control More freely lowered Sanctums allow greater influence over many followers.
Transformation Sufficiently lowered Sanctums permit changes to souls and, consequently, bodies.
Consumption Permanently destroys followers' souls to extend the Stewards' lives.
Political method Manufacture war and desperation to persuade populations to accept their rule voluntarily.
Homeworld An apparent utopia whose buildings are constructed from the remains of consumed followers.

The Stewards' central vulnerability is consent at scale: they cannot compel entire civilizations to lower their Sanctums, so they engineer circumstances in which people do so willingly. Their ability to forcibly breach a few raised Sanctums does not grant unlimited coercive reach.

IV. The Queen and Her Colony

The Queen is an ancient, soul-bearing insectoid entity whose innate abilities include creating soulless insectoid life, Preservation, and Restoration. Her colony functions through independent insect instincts and a biological sensory link to its creator.

Collective Sight — The Thousand Eyes

  • Passive awareness: Insects communicate primitive impressions of movement, threats, and unusual events.
  • Focused sight: The Queen can perceive through a chosen subject's senses.
  • Collective observation: Scouts survey distant regions and help identify possible Champions.
  • Limit: Scouts must physically reach a place; they do not automatically read souls or penetrate the Sovereign Veil.

Soul continuity

  • The Queen establishes Preservation while the subject is alive.
  • At death, a previously preserved soul retains the capacity for Restoration.
  • Souls already Retained by the Archivist cannot be retroactively preserved or restored.
  • Preservation does not automatically grant the Queen the subject's consent, loyalty, or Sovereignty.
  • The Queen's methods do not require physically eating subjects; older descriptions of her consuming bodies to preserve them are retired.

The Queen's motives remain opaque. Her preserved Champions can return after a party wipe, supporting the game's roguelike run structure.

V. The Ancient Kingdom — An Oathbreaker-Related History

The ancient human kingdom arose on an Earth-like planet. Long before its destruction, an anomalous encounter with the Archivist's old-man manifestation introduced its ruler to the principles of Will; the Archivist offered knowledge and direction rather than ruling the kingdom.

Over generations, the kingdom's scholars refined Will and developed the royal inheritance called Ancestral Dominion. This was an extraordinary capacity to assert authority over eligible retained souls, not a form of the Stewards' Siphoning.

The Convergence and genocide

The kingdom's planet was caught in a Rift Convergence event. One of the newly opened Rifts happened to connect it to the Stewards' world, exposing the kingdom to their civilization and, eventually, their ambitions.

The Stewards identified the kingdom's mastery of Will and soul Sovereignty as a threat to their system of control through lowered Sanctums. During the chaos, they launched a deliberate genocide against the kingdom and the people preserving its knowledge. The Sovereign Veil did not cause the attack.

[Open] The exact process by which the Stewards learned of the kingdom's techniques, and whether they initially posed as benefactors, has not been established.

The final contingency

In the kingdom's last hours, the reigning king, royal family, and surviving scholars enacted the unfinished Sovereign Veil. They sought to conceal the Archivist and the Ancestral Domain from the Stewards and save the spiritual inheritance of the royal line.

  • Concealment: Hides the Archivist, Ancestral Domain, and their spiritual connections from the Stewards.
  • Retention filter: Restricts future retention to the designated royal bloodline, reducing detectable spiritual activity.
  • Inheritance filter: Restricts the royal descendant's access to the authorized ancestral inheritance, not the Archivist's older Library.
  • Emergency: The kingdom's dying souls were retained during the final contingency, including some non-royal individuals; this does not automatically grant the Oathbreaker command over them.
  • Limitations: The Veil was untested, unfinished, lacked redundancy, and had no completed reversal spell.

The Queen is also unable to perceive the hidden Domain, an unintended consequence. Even if she could see it, the souls already under Retention lack the continuity needed for Restoration.

VI. The Archivist, the Library, and The Codex

Term Meaning

The Library Its original pre-Veil collection of real, conscious, knowledgeable souls.
Ancestral Domain The concealed inheritance associated with the destroyed kingdom and its bloodline.
Sovereign Veil Artificial overlay limiting the Archivist's future retention and concealing the Domain.
The Codex Physical manifestation of the Archivist that requires a wielder to act.
Ancestral Dominion The Oathbreaker's inherited authority to command eligible ancestral souls.

The Archivist cannot directly conduct campaigns in the physical world or wield The Codex independently. Its old-man form influences through teaching; its sword form provides a physical conduit for an eligible wielder.
